## A4. Numerical method and sensitivity
**Verified benchmark source:** Tennis Abstract WTA Elo, updated 9 November 2026: Hercog overall Elo 1528.3 and cElo 1510.8; Lepchenko overall Elo 1547.9 and cElo 1515.3. Under PROBABILITY_TOOLKIT §6, blended surface ratings are 1519.55 Hercog and 1531.60 Lepchenko. Hence 

`p_ELO(Hercog) = 1 / (1 + 10^((1531.60-1519.55)/400)) = 0.4826656059`;
`p_ELO(Lepchenko) = 0.5173343941`.

**Critical distinction:** These are a rating-reference calculation only, **not** a frozen, issued `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1` match probability, and not a coherent score-distribution output. Tennis Abstract's separately published tournament-draw benchmark indicates 50.6% Hercog and 49.4% Lepchenko, reinforcing that both ratings/benchmark mechanisms are effectively a coin flip rather than demonstrating a meaningful edge. Do not use independent predictors as features in the card. https://tennisabstract.com/reports/wta_elo_ratings.html ; https://www.tennisabstract.com/current/2026WTASopo125.html .

**Point-to-match / set-score tree:** NOT_COMPUTED. Current surface-, opponent- and event-level numerators and denominators for each player's serve and return points and validated opponent-adjusted hold rates are not available in comparable form. Official WTA site has some Lepchenko cumulative stats but Hercog 2026 match-level stats are unavailable there. A fabricated per-game hold, fitted shock width, or hard-coded set-score branch is prohibited. Tier C: reference NONE. League baseline at exact handicaps/totals: BASELINE_UNAVAILABLE. Adjustments: none numerically entered. Sensitivity: Hercog's better clay and recent record may exceed Lepchenko's small Elo edge; unverified physical condition or atypical altitude could reverse either qualitative direction. No numerical confidence interval is asserted.

Expected games Hercog/Lepchenko, expected total games, standard deviations, match game margin and probability of 3 sets: NOT_ESTIMATED. A benchmark winner-only number cannot identify any of these.

## Decision summary

- **Likely winner:** Perth Lynx, direction supported by stronger W–L record and home court, but exact official model probability `NOT_ESTIMATED`.
- **Highest indicative contract:** Bendigo Spirit **+10.5**, *tentative, low-to-medium confidence*. The unqualified shrunk-scores diagnostic gives ~61.6% cover versus Perth −10.5 ~38.4%. This is **not a certified success probability**; it is highly sensitive to Bendigo's final active roster.
- **Total 167.5:** **no reliable side**. The simple current-season diagnostic gives Over ~55.2% / Under ~44.8%, while six prior H2Hs all finished Under and a plausible Bendigo availability reduction reverses the Over lean. **Abstain** for a strong total selection.
- **Formal Rank-1 status:** `NOT_ASSESSABLE / NO_STRONG_PICK`; no issued ranks, no pre-event frozen card.

## A4. Arithmetic — independent illustrative diagnostic, not repository model execution

Current team points from first four/three results and available nine-team 2026–27 league standings were independently reconciled. The complete indexed standings snapshot indicated cumulative WNBL points-for 3,434 across 42 team-games = 21 fixtures, league mean total 163.52, average team score 81.7619. This league mean is **provisional** because the provider's direct page sometimes renders an earlier snapshot; recompute from the official full 21-game ledger before any formal reference freeze. Standings snapshot: https://www.fanziz.com/basketball/series/wnbl-2627/points-table. Match-result corroboration: https://api.thesportsdb.com/season/5113-australian-wnbl/2026-2027.

Direct two-sided offence/defence mean diagnostic: Perth=(87.33+89.75)/2=88.54; Bendigo=(78.25+88.00)/2=83.13; total=171.67; Perth margin=5.42. This treats only three/four games as fully trustworthy and therefore is not the selected estimator.

**Shrinkage demonstrator:** fixed prior equivalent to `k=5` league games, not fitted or validated. Perth weight `wP=3/(3+5)=0.375`, Bendigo weight `wB=4/(4+5)=0.4444`. Let `L=163.5238/2=81.7619` team points. For each score, add its own shrunk offence departure and opponent shrunk defence departure. Split an **assumed** home advantage of +1.5 points in margin into +0.75/-0.75, taken for scenario illustration from the repository's FIBA40 family home-prior +1.49 (not WNBL-validated):

- `E(Perth)=L+wP×(87.333−L)+wB×(89.75−L)+0.75 = 88.1515`.
- `E(Bendigo)=L+wB×(78.25−L)+wP×(88−L)−0.75 = 81.7903`.
- `E(total)=169.9418`; `E(Perth margin)=+6.3611`.

Width floor proxy: `sigma_margin=12.76×1.10=14.036` and `sigma_total=16.82×1.10=18.502` from `PARAMETERS.md` FIBA40 family residual widths. WNBL-specific residuals, covariance, possessions and efficiency are NOT_ESTIMATED. For an illustrative normal marginal only: `P(Perth ML)≈Phi(6.3611/14.036)=0.6748`, `P(Perth -10.5)≈Phi((6.3611-10.5)/14.036)=0.3840`, `P(Bendigo +10.5)≈0.6160`, `P(Over 167.5)≈Phi((169.9418-167.5)/18.502)=0.5525`, `P(Under 167.5)≈0.4475`.

**Limitations:** these normal probabilities are hand-calculated (no MC or fitted engine), and are **not** published p_card or qualified probabilities. A fully coherent joint team-score distribution, actual WNBL possession-based ORtg/DRtg, covariance, overtime branch, confirmed lineup-specific effects and precision audit have not been computed. An approximation to completed-game averages is not an explicitly simulated overtime process. Under the repository, numerical gate, pair rank and joint-failure results are NOT_ASSESSABLE.

## A5. Complete supplied-contract assessment and sensitivity

| Scenario | Expected Perth | Expected Bendigo | Mean total | Mean home margin | Indicative P(Bendigo +10.5) | Indicative P(Over 167.5) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| More current-season weight (`k=3`) | 89.86 | 82.12 | 171.99 | +7.74 | 57.8% | 59.6% |
| Base illustrative (`k=5`) | 88.15 | 81.79 | 169.94 | +6.36 | 61.6% | 55.2% |
| More league shrinkage (`k=7`) | 87.09 | 81.61 | 168.69 | +5.48 | 64.0% | 52.6% |
| More shrinkage (`k=10`) | 86.08 | 81.45 | 167.53 | +4.63 | 66.2% | 50.1% |

**Availability conditional sensitivity, NOT empirical effects:** shifting Bendigo's base centre down 3 points (e.g., less creator/bench availability) changes its indicative +10.5 cover to 53.2% and Over to 48.8%. A −5-point Bendigo change yields about 47.6% and 44.5%, reversing the cover preference. A +3 change raises them to 69.4% and 61.6%. These are thought experiments, not estimates that an individual absence is worth 3 or 5 points.

**Interpretation:** Lynx winner is the broad directional call. Bendigo +10.5 is the leading conditional spread lean if the last-game rotation is retained; it is not a strong unconditional play because the exact-day absences are disputed. At 167.5, competing current-season rates and six older H2Hs do not support a stable total lean.

In the absence of a supported joint team-score model, `P(both fail)` = **NOT_COMPUTED**. Illustrative margin and total normal marginals alone cannot certify that joint.

## A4. Numerical attempts and reference suitability

### Historical repo league profile vs current league

The repository's 22 Oct 2026 KBL historical parameter row states mean total 159.39, home margin +1.18, residual margin SD 12.61 and total SD 16.82 points (`PARAMETERS.md`, Part A). However, the October-November 2026–27 league is scoring more: summing the published team *points-for* in the 10-team current standings (10,466 points across 122 team games, equivalent to 61 scheduled games) yields 171.57 points per game. That aggregate is a cross-sectional **approximation**, not a validated point-in-time official league baseline. Data: https://www.linevs.com/stats/?left_country=South+Korea&left_league=KBL&tab=teams . Source table may be refreshed after the issue horizon, so prospective admissibility is **not established**.

The 12/13-game team scoring midpoint is:
- Mobis expected from own offense versus opponent allowance = (83.5 + 90.8) / 2 = **87.15**.
- Goyang expected from own offense versus opponent allowance = (89.6 + 88.7) / 2 = **89.15**.
- Exploratory total = **176.30** points; Goyang expected lead **2.00** points, before proper opponent strength, home, player availability and current 3P effects.

Recent-form (L5) midpoint, highly exposed to pre-injury regimes:
- Mobis (79.0 + 99.6) / 2 = 89.3; Goyang (95.4 + 89.2) / 2 = 92.3; exploratory total 181.6 and Goyang +3.0.

Current home/away alternate midpoint based on published rounded splits:
- Mobis home 84.0 scored, Goyang away 88.9 conceded → Mobis 86.45;
- Goyang away 85.9 scored, Mobis home 88.4 conceded → Goyang 87.15;
- Alternative total 173.60 and Goyang +0.70, before player revisions.

Thus even **before injury adjustment**, reasonable simple calculations span totals ~173.6–181.6 and away leads ~0.7–3.0. Because the available data do not establish a measured effect size for a foreign-player absence or a validated matchup-specific pace × efficiency distribution, do **not** arbitrarily subtract a fixed number of points and call the result the `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1` forecast.

### Transparency-only numeric calculation (NOT PRIMARY)

As a **demonstration only**, treating integer margin and total as marginal Normal variables around the *unadjusted season midpoint*, widening historical KBL residual SDs by 10% to represent uncertainty, gives margin SD ~13.87, total SD ~18.50. The probabilities below are not a complete joint possession-grid forecast; margin/total covariance, discrete overtime outcome paths, adjusted player minutes and calibration are missing. Approximate integer continuity cells for the 3-point spread are used, but are not validated model probabilities.

| Demonstration | Conditional decisive estimate | Limitation |
|---|---:|---|
| Goyang match win (winner margin ≥1) | ≈54.3% | simple unadjusted score-margin model |
| Mobis match win | ≈45.7% | same model, no team draw after OT |
| Mobis +3.0 cover given decisive | ≈53.0% | 2.9% illustrative exact-three PUSH mass |
| Goyang −3.0 cover given decisive | ≈47.0% | same push mass |
| Over 175.5 | ≈51.7% | unadjusted total normal, without availability |
| Under 175.5 | ≈48.3% | complement |

## A5. Reference stage, score process, exact arithmetic and sensitivity

**Official repository `ml` reference:** `NOT_RUN`, because no checked-out writable repository and no authorized writer. NHL requires a league-only baseline, not an unpromoted fitted-model reference. Published `PARAMETERS.md` Part A NHL family has regular-season mean total **6.23**, raw total SD 2.30, residual total SD 2.30 and residual margin SD 2.55. Do not label the following `p_card` or `BASELINE_P`; no exact registered reference receipt was executed.

**Transparent Wolfram numerical diagnostic (run, but never as the repository model):**

- Official season scoring GF/GA: COL 72/18 GF, 55/18 GA; TOR 64/19 GF, 63/19 GA. The two cross-team unadjusted mids are `muCOL = (72/18 + 63/19)/2 = 3.657895` and `muTOR = (64/19 + 55/18)/2 = 3.211988`.
- Declared **illustrative**, untrained shrink coefficient 0.50 toward league-neutral 6.23/2 = 3.115 per team; illustrative Toronto home-edge +0.13 net (±0.065): `lambda_COL = 3.321447`, `lambda_TOR = 3.228494` goals in a *regulation proxy* model. The season scores include some OT; hence treating them as clean regulation rates is an approximation, not a correct issued primary model.
- For each 60-minute score `i,j` in 0..23, assign `p_ij=Poisson(lambda_COL,i)*Poisson(lambda_TOR,j)`. For each tie `i=j`, allocate one additional deciding goal to COL or TOR at a declared **unvalidated 50/50** OT/SO decision, on endpoints `(i+1,j)` and `(i,j+1)` respectively. No independent market quotes, no outcomes and no fitted/retrained engine used. Tail (24+ goals) contributes below the reported precision and was separately checked at 0..29. A separate pull-goalie/empty-net state was **not identified**, so the resulting puckline mass is illustrative and *not* an issued rank.
- Probability mass: `P(COL ML)=0.514202`, `P(TOR ML)=0.485798`, `P(TOR+1.5)=0.713790`, `P(COL−1.5)=0.286210`, `P(O6.5)=0.530312`, `P(U6.5)=0.469688`. Pair sums = 1 to numerical tolerance.
- Regulation tie mass `0.159070`; model final means COL `3.4010` and TOR `3.3080` (total `6.7090`, margin `0.0930`). This is **not a calibrated score forecast**.
- Goalie quality and injury differences are directional evidence but have `MEASURED_EFFECT_NOT_AVAILABLE`; neither is silently converted to a numeric goal shift.

Toronto +1.5 covers all one-goal wins/losses but fails on a two-goal Colorado margin, frequently caused by a late empty net. Indoor rink; weather `N/A`. Officiating crew `NOT_VERIFIED`.

## A5. Mechanism, matchup, expected goal process

- **Scoring strength:** Abbotsford offense 3.50 GF/g versus Colorado's 2.50 GA/g, opposing strength Colorado offense 2.86 GF/g versus Abbotsford's 2.67 GA/g. These are descriptive season estimates and reflect unverified opponent schedules; they do not measure underlying shot quality.
- **Rest/travel:** Both last completed Nov 7 local (Nov 8 some UTC), about 3 local days rest; Colorado travelled to Abbotsford; Abbotsford at home for second consecutive series. Exact travel and fatigue effect NOT_ESTIMATED.
- **Home:** Abbotsford 5–1 home record; Colorado 3–3 road record through cutoff, but these samples are too small to measure an AHL home-margin effect. The +/-0.12 goal shift below is explicitly a scenario assumption, not an empirical adjustment.
- **Goalies:** Expected Nabokov versus Tolopilo; both high recent save-percentages, competing with Abbotsford’s higher offensive rate. If either backup starts, centre and widths could change. No named starting goalie confirmed, so this should not be a confident rank.
- **Special teams:** First Abbotsford loss of streak was 0–3 vs Ontario despite five failed power plays; newly deployed PP1 defenseman Thompson, recently returned Cootes. Colorado's Nov 6 game had 1-for-7 power play and 5-for-6 penalty kill; these are one-game rates, not calibrated team-level adjustments. Source club match recap https://www.coloradoeagles.com/news/detail/colorado-tripped-up-by-wranglers-2-1 . The older H2H shows Lind twice scored in 4-3 Abbotsford win; roster and opponent-adjustments may differ now. Sources https://www.coloradoeagles.com/news/detail/eagles-come-up-short-in-4-3-loss-to-abbotsford and https://www.coloradoeagles.com/news/detail/eagles-roar-back-to-defeat-abbotsford-4-3-in-shootout .
- **Kill paths:** Abbotsford ML fails if Colorado's better goalie performance and low-event defensive play dominate; Over 5.5 fails if both starting goalies limit high-danger chances and the game resembles recent 2-1/0-3 games. Under 5.5 fails if Abbotsford's return-to-lineup forwards, special teams and extra-attacker tail raise the combined goals.

## A6. Transparent independent calculation — NOT a repository reference, NOT `p_card`

The competition has **no verified AHL-specific parameter row** in PARAMETERS Part A. Repository baseline reference command and promoted model were **NOT_RUN** (not available in this chat, also no writer authority). The computation below is an **UNCALIBRATED_DIAGNOSTIC** based on explicitly stated assumptions, not a model execution or calibration certificate.

1. Team rates from exact pre-cutoff results: raw cross-matched regulation-goal proxies `uCOL=((40/14)+(32/12))/2 = 2.761905`, `uABB=((42/12)+(35/14))/2 = 3.000000`. These source scores include shootout bookkeeping and therefore are not clean regulation-only rates; this is an approximation, labelled.
2. Illustrative current AHL league goal-total prior `uL=5.83`, one-team mean `2.915`. Shrink each team centre 50% toward the population: `vCOL=(uCOL+2.915)/2 = 2.838452`, `vABB=(uABB+2.915)/2 = 2.957500`. Shrinkage percentage 50% is analyst scenario (NOT_FITTED). This is not a league-certified baseline.
3. Illustrative home separation: subtract 0.12 from Colorado and add 0.12 to Abbotsford: `muCOL=2.718452`, `muABB=3.077500`, total **5.795952**, home expected margin **0.359048**. The +/-0.12 is NOT measured in AHL; see zero/stronger cases. Do not misrepresent as an adjustment ledger FULL effect.
4. Independent regulation Poisson `C ~ Pois(muCOL)`, `A ~ Pois(muABB)`. Probability `P(C wins full)=P(C>A)+0.5P(C=A)`; `P(A wins full)=P(A>C)+0.5P(C=A)`. Fifty-fifty overtime assignment is illustrative, not historical AHL calibration. `P(Over5.5)=P(C+A >=6)=1−PoissonCDF(5,muCOL+muABB)`; full-game shootout/tiebreak adds one goal to tied even totals, but this does **not** change the Over 5.5 boundary (4->5 remains Under; 6->7 remains Over). Explicit regulation tie probability `0.168012`; entire tie mass is redistributed to one-goal full-match wins. Empty-net tail, goalie mixture, shot covariance and individual replacement minutes are NOT_MODELED.
5. Exact independently executed `scipy.stats` computations (not repo `ml.reference` or a Monte Carlo): `P(ABB ML)=0.557987`; `P(COL ML)=0.442013`; `P(Over5.5)=0.521015`; `P(Under5.5)=0.478985`. Complements sum 1; no push/void under assumed action. `BASELINE_LEAGUE_V1` NOT_COMPUTED; simple *unverified* league-rate Poisson comparator `P(Over5.5;lambda5.83)=0.526643` is diagnostic only.
6. Regulation independent-Poisson model has `E[reg total]=5.7960`. Under the illustrative exactly-one-goal rule for regulation ties, `E[full total]=5.9640`. Expected final by team approximately shifts +0.5 tie probability each, `COL=2.8025`, `ABB=3.1615`; these score centres are sensitivity diagnostics, not an observed or operator-verified endpoint.

### Sensitivity (assumptions, not confidence intervals)

| Scenario | COL regulation goal mean | ABB regulation goal mean | P(ABB ML) | P(Over 5.5) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| No quantified home effect | 2.838 | 2.958 | 51.9% | 52.1% |
| Illustrative ±0.12 home adjustment | 2.718 | 3.078 | 55.8% | 52.1% |
| Illustrative ±0.25 home adjustment | 2.588 | 3.208 | 59.9% | 52.1% |
| Lower-event goalie scenario | 2.550 | 2.800 | 54.2% | 44.5% |
| Higher-event game scenario | 3.000 | 3.400 | 56.2% | 61.6% |

**Joint exposure:** Under base exact grid, `P(ABB ML AND Over5.5)=0.29556`, `P(ABB ML AND Under5.5)=0.26243`, `P(COL ML AND Over5.5)=0.22545`, `P(COL ML AND Under5.5)=0.21656`; total 1 within numerical tolerance. `P(both picks fail if ABB ML + Over5.5)=0.21656`; no claim this pairing is independent or statistically profitable. Player-status shocks can correlate goals, winner and total.

## A7. Decision, ranking and contrary cases

- Directional moneyline lean: **Abbotsford Canucks ML**, from the 8–3–1 record, strong 3.5 GF/g, home and continuity of Cootes/Thompson. Exact model 55.8% is **illustrative**, and no valid `p_card` is published.
- Total 5.5: **NO_STRONG_PICK / BALANCED**; 52.1% Over under selected assumptions, 44.5% Over in low-scoring goalie case versus 61.6% in high-event case. Neither number establishes a verified >65% edge; no choice on total under the formal rule.
- Most-likely *diagnostic* row is `ABB ML` followed by `Over5.5`, but this is not an issued `PICKS_V2` ranking and no formal strong selection passes. The H2H 7,7 total can overfit a two-game sample. There is no clean claim of matchday lineup confirmation.

## A5. Basketball process, baseline, equations and sensitivity

**This section is a hand/Wolfram-executed independent approximation, not `ml.reference`, not an official `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1`, and not a calibrated model.** No market prices, prediction site models or tipster estimates are admitted. It prices every supplied line from one joint *illustrative* normal score construction.

1. 2025–26 NCAA D-I men's per-team scoring mean = 75.9, 365 teams (Sports Reference historical season table): https://www.sports-reference.com/cbb/seasons/game-averages.html . So generic two-team population centre = **151.8**. This is historical *baseline centre only*, not a full distributional fit.
2. Two-game cross-matched raw scoring centres: Creighton scored 79.5, Kansas State allowed 73.0 → μ_C_raw=(79.5+73.0)/2=**76.25**. Kansas State scored 80.5, Creighton allowed 58.0 → μ_K_raw=(80.5+58.0)/2=**69.25**; total 145.5, Creighton margin +7.0. These are strongly schedule-confounded (Kansas City/Le Moyne vs SDSU/CSUN).
3. Transparent *assumed* shrinkage n/(n+k) with n=2 games and k=4 prior-equivalent games, w=2/(2+4)=**1/3**. Total μ_T=(2/3)×151.8+(1/3)×145.5=**149.7**. No validated evidence that k=4 is optimal.
4. Venue prior CREI margin **−2.5** as an explicit unmeasured assumption, then margin μ_M=(2/3)×(−2.5)+(1/3)×(+7.0)=**+0.667 Creighton**. Projected score μ_C=(149.7+0.667)/2=**75.18**, μ_K=**74.52**.
5. Set illustrative σ_total=**21.0 points**, σ_margin=**14.5 points**, intentionally broad because of two-game profiles, shooting volatility and unfamiliar rotations. These are assumptions, **not measured NCAA residuals or repo width floors**. Model independent total and margin normals T~N(149.7,21²), M=Creighton−KansasState~N(0.667,14.5²), projected team scores C=(T+M)/2, K=(T−M)/2. Consequently team score covariance Cov(C,K)=(21²−14.5²)/4=**57.69 points²**; no separate cross-team covariance fit. Negative/joint impossible-score tails are a limitation of the normal approximation.
6. Φ is standard normal CDF. Winner P(CREI)=Φ(μ_M/14.5)=**0.51834**, P(KSU)=0.48166. Spread P(CREI −3.5)=1−Φ((3.5−μ_M)/14.5)=**0.42254**; KSU +3.5 = **0.57746**. Under 159.5 = Φ((159.5−149.7)/21)=**0.67963**; Over=**0.32037**. Complementary rows sum to one; half lines cannot push.
7. Independent league-centre comparator only: if total centre were 151.8 with the same *assumed* 21 width, P(Under159.5)=**0.64307**, which is below the illustrative 0.67963. This is **not** a league-validated BASELINE_P, because width, residual distribution and exact NCAA cohort checks are unavailable.
8. Overtime: the target includes every overtime; historical 75.9 per-team scoring is observed final scoring and includes OTs as they occurred. A separate explicit regulation-tie transition was not fitted. Any double-counting or missing overtime tail is an unresolved method defect; the normal is a rough approximation, not a compliance-certified discrete score model.

**Sensitivity:** Keeping σ_total 21 fixed, P(Under 159.5) = **0.784** if true centre 143; **0.680** at centre 149.7; **0.472** at centre 161. Thus no calibrated 65%+ confidence assertion. Keeping σ_margin 14.5, KSU +3.5 is especially sensitive to home advantage and the uncertain roster. A higher-tempo three-point-heavy state threatens Under and benefits K-State's upset path; Creighton's pressure, forced turnovers and defensive rebounds threaten K-State +3.5 and favour Under. Do not count KSU's 17 made threes and Rechsteiner's 26 points against CSUN as independent separate effects; they overlap.

**Mechanisms that remain unfitted:** game-level possession counts from each opponent, minutes x lineup contributions, shooting attempt denominators beyond KSU 17/41 threes, force-turnover rates opponent-adjusted, rebound/FT rates and endgame foul pressure, venue effect measured prospectively, late roster confirmation, overtime-tie probability. No claim of 100,000 Monte Carlo trials or model execution.

## A6. Tempo, form, coaching and rules audit

- **NCAAB format:** 40-minute game, two 20-minute halves; five-minute overtime as required; user contracts are full-game including OT under repository's BK-OT-1 convention. College rules differ from NBA 48-minute assumptions and FIBA quarter-based play.
- **Perimeter and tempo:** K-State's 17 made threes on school-record 41 attempts vs CSUN, and first-two-game 34.5 attempted threes/game and 12.5 makes, establish an elevated high-variance offensive mechanism. Not sustainable at identical conversion against a stronger defence without evidence.
- **Creighton defence:** forced 22 Kansas City turnovers with 15 steals and 11 steals in game 2 per Creighton report. The two opponents were both weaker than Kansas State; these rates are not opponent adjusted.
- **Schedule:** both teams have multi-day recovery, Creighton first travel and Kansas State home. No demonstrated back-to-back fatigue or altitude factor.
- **Coaches:** Alan Huss (former High Point coach) is in his first Creighton season; Casey Alexander (formerly Belmont) is in his first Kansas State season. Kansas State roster essentially new; Creighton eight to nine newcomers dominate usage. Prior-season H2H not current systems.
- **Spread kill path:** K-State's threes break a tie or Creighton offensive stagnation makes a 4+ point Kansas State win; Creighton winning narrowly still covers Kansas State +3.5. Kansas State +3.5 loses if Creighton wins by 4+.
- **Under kill path:** three-point barrage, strong transition scoring, repeated bonus FTs, endgame intentional fouls, OT. Under strengthened by Creighton turnovers forced, longer defensive possessions, three-point regression.
- **Correlation:** Both sides and total derive from the same illustrative bivariate normal score object; exact joint failure of Under and KSU +3.5 is `NOT_COMPUTED`. They cannot be treated as independent safeguards. A high K-State three-point shooting state tends to endanger Under but help the KSU cushion. These are conditional, not measured dependence claims.

## A7. Decision and conditional scenarios

- **Preferred provided market:** Under 159.5, **tentative**; true evidence outside the two-game sample is thin and both new coaches can change tempo. The score-centre approximation is 75–75, so Under wins in the base scenario, but this is a diagnostic only.
- **Spread:** K-State +3.5 preferred to Creighton −3.5 only directionally. Two-point or three-point KSU losses also cover; a four-point Creighton win does not.
- **Moneyline:** numerical approximation is near 52/48 Creighton; **no persuasive outright edge**. A precise team edge is not established by the 2–0 versus 1–1 record.
- **Formal status:** `ABSTAIN / NO_STRONG_PICK` despite p_Under≈0.68 in the assumed normal: confidence is not qualified, missing lineup/minutes/tier baseline/independent opponent adjustment and timing freeze invalidate a primary promoted claim. No certainty threshold should be inferred from shadow diagnostic alone.

## A5. Reference attempt, baseline arithmetic, coherent contract approximation

**Actual formal code execution:** `ml reference tier`: NOT_RUN; `ml pregame reference`: NOT_RUN; `SR-KIT-v24.15` primary: NOT_RUN; `ml external init/merge`: NOT_RUN. Inputs: Akita 82.7 PF, 90.1 PA (n15); Ibaraki 83.6 PF, 78.4 PA (n16). Last ten Akita 89.0 PF/93.4 PA; Ibaraki 86.7 PF/74.6 PA. Data are current-season only. Cross-match as (own attack + opponent defence)/2; blend **2/3 season, 1/3 L10** as an illustrative sensitivity (L10 overlaps season; this is not two independent samples). Add **+0.305 to home** and **−0.305 to away** (0.61-point home margin, broader v24.15 B.League family proxy, not exact B.PREMIER adjudication).

```
season Akita = (82.7 + 78.4) / 2 = 80.55
season Ibaraki = (83.6 + 90.1) / 2 = 86.85
L10 Akita = (89.0 + 74.6) / 2 = 81.80
L10 Ibaraki = (86.7 + 93.4) / 2 = 90.05
Akita centre = (2*80.55 + 81.80)/3 + 0.305 = 81.2716667
Ibaraki centre = (2*86.85 + 90.05)/3 - 0.305 = 87.6116667
TOTAL centre = 168.8833333
MARGIN (Akita - Ibaraki) centre = -6.34
Proxy SD margin = 14.94; proxy SD total = 17.66 (general B.League development family; B.PREMIER calibration absent)
```

**Arithmetic:** normal CDF directly from SciPy in a coherent bivariate normal score proxy, using independent margin and total coordinates (not a possession-level joint grid), without integer discretisation or an explicit real-overtime branch. Because the two coordinate distributions share the same expected score centre, each side's and each total's complement agree. Continuity and OT approximation are a real limitation. The probability figures are illustrative only, not independently validated.

## A6. Contract assessment, alternative explanations and dependence

- **Hiroshima -2.5:** 13–1 vs 12–4, four straight wins, Cockburn inside and home/rest support; however the illustrative expected margin is only +2.36, just below the −2.5 threshold; not a strong preference. Loss path is Hiroshima winning by 1 or 2, or losing.
- **Sendai +2.5:** strong 3-point volume and 12–4 form allow close game, and covers if Sendai wins or Hiroshima wins by 1 or 2. Hiroshima's large interior advantage could break that cushion. Diagnostic roughly 50.4%, no material margin edge.
- **Under 170.5:** highest conditional normal diagnostic ~54.6%; Hiroshima L5 165.2, Sendai L5 169.8, older H2H 169.25, both defences under 80 season PA. Risk: Culver 27 PPG, Sendai 12.6 threes, possible foul/OT pace tail. No robust advantage.
- **Over 170.5:** offensive season scoring (90.4+87.6=178.0 without matchup correction) is the strongest contrary case, as is Sendai's explosive 107 points in latest game. Season cross-matching suppresses mean to 168.45. Neither method independently qualified.
- All four supplied rows are covered by illustrative diagnostic, but **zero** receives a formal `PICKS_V2` number. ML comparisons are not owner's supplied side contracts. No multi-leg proposition or claimed dependence calculation.

- All four supplied rows are covered by illustrative diagnostic, but **zero** receives a formal `PICKS_V2` number. ML comparisons are not owner's supplied side contracts. No multi-leg proposition or claimed dependence calculation.

## A5. Mechanism, matchup, expected goal process

- **Scoring strength:** Abbotsford offense 3.50 GF/g versus Colorado's 2.50 GA/g, opposing strength Colorado offense 2.86 GF/g versus Abbotsford's 2.67 GA/g. These are descriptive season estimates and reflect unverified opponent schedules; they do not measure underlying shot quality.
- **Rest/travel:** Both last completed Nov 7 local (Nov 8 some UTC), about 3 local days rest; Colorado travelled to Abbotsford; Abbotsford at home for second consecutive series. Exact travel and fatigue effect NOT_ESTIMATED.
- **Home:** Abbotsford 5–1 home record; Colorado 3–3 road record through cutoff, but these samples are too small to measure an AHL home-margin effect. The +/-0.12 goal shift below is explicitly a scenario assumption, not an empirical adjustment.
- **Goalies:** Expected Nabokov versus Tolopilo; both high recent save-percentages, competing with Abbotsford’s higher offensive rate. If either backup starts, centre and widths could change. No named starting goalie confirmed, so this should not be a confident rank.
- **Special teams:** First Abbotsford loss of streak was 0–3 vs Ontario despite five failed power plays; newly deployed PP1 defenseman Thompson, recently returned Cootes. Colorado's Nov 6 game had 1-for-7 power play and 5-for-6 penalty kill; these are one-game rates, not calibrated team-level adjustments. Source club match recap https://www.coloradoeagles.com/news/detail/colorado-tripped-up-by-wranglers-2-1 . The older H2H shows Lind twice scored in 4-3 Abbotsford win; roster and opponent-adjustments may differ now. Sources https://www.coloradoeagles.com/news/detail/eagles-come-up-short-in-4-3-loss-to-abbotsford and https://www.coloradoeagles.com/news/detail/eagles-roar-back-to-defeat-abbotsford-4-3-in-shootout .
- **Kill paths:** Abbotsford ML fails if Colorado's better goalie performance and low-event defensive play dominate; Over 5.5 fails if both starting goalies limit high-danger chances and the game resembles recent 2-1/0-3 games. Under 5.5 fails if Abbotsford's return-to-lineup forwards, special teams and extra-attacker tail raise the combined goals.

## A6. Transparent independent calculation — NOT a repository reference, NOT `p_card`

The competition has **no verified AHL-specific parameter row** in PARAMETERS Part A. Repository baseline reference command and promoted model were **NOT_RUN** (not available in this chat, also no writer authority). The computation below is an **UNCALIBRATED_DIAGNOSTIC** based on explicitly stated assumptions, not a model execution or calibration certificate.

1. Team rates from exact pre-cutoff results: raw cross-matched regulation-goal proxies `uCOL=((40/14)+(32/12))/2 = 2.761905`, `uABB=((42/12)+(35/14))/2 = 3.000000`. These source scores include shootout bookkeeping and therefore are not clean regulation-only rates; this is an approximation, labelled.
2. Illustrative current AHL league goal-total prior `uL=5.83`, one-team mean `2.915`. Shrink each team centre 50% toward the population: `vCOL=(uCOL+2.915)/2 = 2.838452`, `vABB=(uABB+2.915)/2 = 2.957500`. Shrinkage percentage 50% is analyst scenario (NOT_FITTED). This is not a league-certified baseline.
3. Illustrative home separation: subtract 0.12 from Colorado and add 0.12 to Abbotsford: `muCOL=2.718452`, `muABB=3.077500`, total **5.795952**, home expected margin **0.359048**. The +/-0.12 is NOT measured in AHL; see zero/stronger cases. Do not misrepresent as an adjustment ledger FULL effect.
4. Independent regulation Poisson `C ~ Pois(muCOL)`, `A ~ Pois(muABB)`. Probability `P(C wins full)=P(C>A)+0.5P(C=A)`; `P(A wins full)=P(A>C)+0.5P(C=A)`. Fifty-fifty overtime assignment is illustrative, not historical AHL calibration. `P(Over5.5)=P(C+A >=6)=1−PoissonCDF(5,muCOL+muABB)`; full-game shootout/tiebreak adds one goal to tied even totals, but this does **not** change the Over 5.5 boundary (4->5 remains Under; 6->7 remains Over). Explicit regulation tie probability `0.168012`; entire tie mass is redistributed to one-goal full-match wins. Empty-net tail, goalie mixture, shot covariance and individual replacement minutes are NOT_MODELED.
5. Exact independently executed `scipy.stats` computations (not repo `ml.reference` or a Monte Carlo): `P(ABB ML)=0.557987`; `P(COL ML)=0.442013`; `P(Over5.5)=0.521015`; `P(Under5.5)=0.478985`. Complements sum 1; no push/void under assumed action. `BASELINE_LEAGUE_V1` NOT_COMPUTED; simple *unverified* league-rate Poisson comparator `P(Over5.5;lambda5.83)=0.526643` is diagnostic only.
6. Regulation independent-Poisson model has `E[reg total]=5.7960`. Under the illustrative exactly-one-goal rule for regulation ties, `E[full total]=5.9640`. Expected final by team approximately shifts +0.5 tie probability each, `COL=2.8025`, `ABB=3.1615`; these score centres are sensitivity diagnostics, not an observed or operator-verified endpoint.

### Sensitivity (assumptions, not confidence intervals)

| Scenario | COL regulation goal mean | ABB regulation goal mean | P(ABB ML) | P(Over 5.5) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| No quantified home effect | 2.838 | 2.958 | 51.9% | 52.1% |
| Illustrative ±0.12 home adjustment | 2.718 | 3.078 | 55.8% | 52.1% |
| Illustrative ±0.25 home adjustment | 2.588 | 3.208 | 59.9% | 52.1% |
| Lower-event goalie scenario | 2.550 | 2.800 | 54.2% | 44.5% |
| Higher-event game scenario | 3.000 | 3.400 | 56.2% | 61.6% |

**Joint exposure:** Under base exact grid, `P(ABB ML AND Over5.5)=0.29556`, `P(ABB ML AND Under5.5)=0.26243`, `P(COL ML AND Over5.5)=0.22545`, `P(COL ML AND Under5.5)=0.21656`; total 1 within numerical tolerance. `P(both picks fail if ABB ML + Over5.5)=0.21656`; no claim this pairing is independent or statistically profitable. Player-status shocks can correlate goals, winner and total.

## A7. Decision, ranking and contrary cases

- Directional moneyline lean: **Abbotsford Canucks ML**, from the 8–3–1 record, strong 3.5 GF/g, home and continuity of Cootes/Thompson. Exact model 55.8% is **illustrative**, and no valid `p_card` is published.
- Total 5.5: **NO_STRONG_PICK / BALANCED**; 52.1% Over under selected assumptions, 44.5% Over in low-scoring goalie case versus 61.6% in high-event case. Neither number establishes a verified >65% edge; no choice on total under the formal rule.
- Most-likely *diagnostic* row is `ABB ML` followed by `Over5.5`, but this is not an issued `PICKS_V2` ranking and no formal strong selection passes. The H2H 7,7 total can overfit a two-game sample. There is no clean claim of matchday lineup confirmation.
- No source-grounded adjustments meeting EFFECT_TABLES timing and independent measurement criteria; all numerical shifts remain explicit scenario assumptions.

## A5. Basketball process, baseline, equations and sensitivity

**This section is a hand/Wolfram-executed independent approximation, not `ml.reference`, not an official `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1`, and not a calibrated model.** No market prices, prediction site models or tipster estimates are admitted. It prices every supplied line from one joint *illustrative* normal score construction.

1. 2025–26 NCAA D-I men's per-team scoring mean = 75.9, 365 teams (Sports Reference historical season table): https://www.sports-reference.com/cbb/seasons/game-averages.html . So generic two-team population centre = **151.8**. This is historical *baseline centre only*, not a full distributional fit.
2. Two-game cross-matched raw scoring centres: Creighton scored 79.5, Kansas State allowed 73.0 → μ_C_raw=(79.5+73.0)/2=**76.25**. Kansas State scored 80.5, Creighton allowed 58.0 → μ_K_raw=(80.5+58.0)/2=**69.25**; total 145.5, Creighton margin +7.0. These are strongly schedule-confounded (Kansas City/Le Moyne vs SDSU/CSUN).
3. Transparent *assumed* shrinkage n/(n+k) with n=2 games and k=4 prior-equivalent games, w=2/(2+4)=**1/3**. Total μ_T=(2/3)×151.8+(1/3)×145.5=**149.7**. No validated evidence that k=4 is optimal.
4. Venue prior CREI margin **−2.5** as an explicit unmeasured assumption, then margin μ_M=(2/3)×(−2.5)+(1/3)×(+7.0)=**+0.667 Creighton**. Projected score μ_C=(149.7+0.667)/2=**75.18**, μ_K=**74.52**.
5. Set illustrative σ_total=**21.0 points**, σ_margin=**14.5 points**, intentionally broad because of two-game profiles, shooting volatility and unfamiliar rotations. These are assumptions, **not measured NCAA residuals or repo width floors**. Model independent total and margin normals T~N(149.7,21²), M=Creighton−KansasState~N(0.667,14.5²), projected team scores C=(T+M)/2, K=(T−M)/2. Consequently team score covariance Cov(C,K)=(21²−14.5²)/4=**57.69 points²**; no separate cross-team covariance fit. Negative/joint impossible-score tails are a limitation of the normal approximation.
6. Φ is standard normal CDF. Winner P(CREI)=Φ(μ_M/14.5)=**0.51834**, P(KSU)=0.48166. Spread P(CREI −3.5)=1−Φ((3.5−μ_M)/14.5)=**0.42254**; KSU +3.5 = **0.57746**. Under 159.5 = Φ((159.5−149.7)/21)=**0.67963**; Over=**0.32037**. Complementary rows sum to one; half lines cannot push.
7. Independent league-centre comparator only: if total centre were 151.8 with the same *assumed* 21 width, P(Under159.5)=**0.64307**, which is below the illustrative 0.67963. This is **not** a league-validated BASELINE_P, because width, residual distribution and exact NCAA cohort checks are unavailable.
8. Overtime: the target includes every overtime; historical 75.9 per-team scoring is observed final scoring and includes OTs as they occurred. A separate explicit regulation-tie transition was not fitted. Any double-counting or missing overtime tail is an unresolved method defect; the normal is a rough approximation, not a compliance-certified discrete score model.

**Sensitivity:** Keeping σ_total 21 fixed, P(Under 159.5) = **0.784** if true centre 143; **0.680** at centre 149.7; **0.472** at centre 161. Thus no calibrated 65%+ confidence assertion. Keeping σ_margin 14.5, KSU +3.5 is especially sensitive to home advantage and the uncertain roster. A higher-tempo three-point-heavy state threatens Under and benefits K-State's upset path; Creighton's pressure, forced turnovers and defensive rebounds threaten K-State +3.5 and favour Under. Do not count KSU's 17 made threes and Rechsteiner's 26 points against CSUN as independent separate effects; they overlap.

**Mechanisms that remain unfitted:** game-level possession counts from each opponent, minutes x lineup contributions, shooting attempt denominators beyond KSU 17/41 threes, force-turnover rates opponent-adjusted, rebound/FT rates and endgame foul pressure, venue effect measured prospectively, late roster confirmation, overtime-tie probability. No claim of 100,000 Monte Carlo trials or model execution.

## A6. Tempo, form, coaching and rules audit

- **NCAAB format:** 40-minute game, two 20-minute halves; five-minute overtime as required; user contracts are full-game including OT under repository's BK-OT-1 convention. College rules differ from NBA 48-minute assumptions and FIBA quarter-based play.
- **Perimeter and tempo:** K-State's 17 made threes on school-record 41 attempts vs CSUN, and first-two-game 34.5 attempted threes/game and 12.5 makes, establish an elevated high-variance offensive mechanism. Not sustainable at identical conversion against a stronger defence without evidence.
- **Creighton defence:** forced 22 Kansas City turnovers with 15 steals and 11 steals in game 2 per Creighton report. The two opponents were both weaker than Kansas State; these rates are not opponent adjusted.
- **Schedule:** both teams have multi-day recovery, Creighton first travel and Kansas State home. No demonstrated back-to-back fatigue or altitude factor.
- **Coaches:** Alan Huss (former High Point coach) is in his first Creighton season; Casey Alexander (formerly Belmont) is in his first Kansas State season. Kansas State roster essentially new; Creighton eight to nine newcomers dominate usage. Prior-season H2H not current systems.
- **Spread kill path:** K-State's threes break a tie or Creighton offensive stagnation makes a 4+ point Kansas State win; Creighton winning narrowly still covers Kansas State +3.5. Kansas State +3.5 loses if Creighton wins by 4+.
- **Under kill path:** three-point barrage, strong transition scoring, repeated bonus FTs, endgame intentional fouls, OT. Under strengthened by Creighton turnovers forced, longer defensive possessions, three-point regression.
- **Correlation:** Both sides and total derive from the same illustrative bivariate normal score object; exact joint failure of Under and KSU +3.5 is `NOT_COMPUTED`. They cannot be treated as independent safeguards. A high K-State three-point shooting state tends to endanger Under but help the KSU cushion. These are conditional, not measured dependence claims.

## A7. Decision and conditional scenarios

- **Preferred provided market:** Under 159.5, **tentative**; true evidence outside the two-game sample is thin and both new coaches can change tempo. The score-centre approximation is 75–75, so Under wins in the base scenario, but this is a diagnostic only.
- **Spread:** K-State +3.5 preferred to Creighton −3.5 only directionally. Two-point or three-point KSU losses also cover; a four-point Creighton win does not.
- **Moneyline:** numerical approximation is near 52/48 Creighton; **no persuasive outright edge**. A precise team edge is not established by the 2–0 versus 1–1 record.
- **Formal status:** `ABSTAIN / NO_STRONG_PICK` despite p_Under≈0.68 in the assumed normal: confidence is not qualified, missing lineup/minutes/tier baseline/independent opponent adjustment and timing freeze invalidate a primary promoted claim. No certainty threshold should be inferred from shadow diagnostic alone.

## A4. Numerical method and sensitivity
**Verified benchmark source:** Tennis Abstract WTA Elo, updated 9 November 2026: Hercog overall Elo 1528.3 and cElo 1510.8; Lepchenko overall Elo 1547.9 and cElo 1515.3. Under PROBABILITY_TOOLKIT §6, blended surface ratings are 1519.55 Hercog and 1531.60 Lepchenko. Hence 

`p_ELO(Hercog) = 1 / (1 + 10^((1531.60-1519.55)/400)) = 0.4826656059`;
`p_ELO(Lepchenko) = 0.5173343941`.

**Critical distinction:** These are a rating-reference calculation only, **not** a frozen, issued `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1` match probability, and not a coherent score-distribution output. Tennis Abstract's separately published tournament-draw benchmark indicates 50.6% Hercog and 49.4% Lepchenko, reinforcing that both ratings/benchmark mechanisms are effectively a coin flip rather than demonstrating a meaningful edge. Do not use independent predictors as features in the card. https://tennisabstract.com/reports/wta_elo_ratings.html ; https://www.tennisabstract.com/current/2026WTASopo125.html .

**Point-to-match / set-score tree:** NOT_COMPUTED. Current surface-, opponent- and event-level numerators and denominators for each player's serve and return points and validated opponent-adjusted hold rates are not available in comparable form. Official WTA site has some Lepchenko cumulative stats but Hercog 2026 match-level stats are unavailable there. A fabricated per-game hold, fitted shock width, or hard-coded set-score branch is prohibited. Tier C: reference NONE. League baseline at exact handicaps/totals: BASELINE_UNAVAILABLE. Adjustments: none numerically entered. Sensitivity: Hercog's better clay and recent record may exceed Lepchenko's small Elo edge; unverified physical condition or atypical altitude could reverse either qualitative direction. No numerical confidence interval is asserted.

Expected games Hercog/Lepchenko, expected total games, standard deviations, match game margin and probability of 3 sets: NOT_ESTIMATED. A benchmark winner-only number cannot identify any of these.

## A5. Reference attempt, baseline arithmetic, coherent contract approximation

**Actual formal code execution:** `ml reference tier`: NOT_RUN; `ml pregame reference`: NOT_RUN; `SR-KIT-v24.15` primary: NOT_RUN; `ml external init/merge`: NOT_RUN. Current competition exact-scope parameter/width is `NOT_VERIFIED`; shared v24.15 model-promotions registry has no rows beyond its header. This file is a **diagnostic normal-CDF calculation performed in the chat's container**, not the official `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1`/`PICKS_V2` primary forecast. No synthetic N=100k Monte Carlo was claimed.

Inputs: Akita 82.7 PF, 90.1 PA (n15); Ibaraki 83.6 PF, 78.4 PA (n16). Last ten Akita 89.0 PF/93.4 PA; Ibaraki 86.7 PF/74.6 PA. Data are current-season only. Cross-match as (own attack + opponent defence)/2; blend **2/3 season, 1/3 L10** as an illustrative sensitivity (L10 overlaps season; this is not two independent samples). Add **+0.305 to home** and **−0.305 to away** (0.61-point home margin, broader v24.15 B.League family proxy, not exact B.PREMIER adjudication).

```
season Akita = (82.7 + 78.4) / 2 = 80.55
season Ibaraki = (83.6 + 90.1) / 2 = 86.85
L10 Akita = (89.0 + 74.6) / 2 = 81.80
L10 Ibaraki = (86.7 + 93.4) / 2 = 90.05
Akita centre = (2*80.55 + 81.80)/3 + 0.305 = 81.2716667
Ibaraki centre = (2*86.85 + 90.05)/3 - 0.305 = 87.6116667
TOTAL centre = 168.8833333
MARGIN (Akita - Ibaraki) centre = -6.34
Proxy SD margin = 14.94; proxy SD total = 17.66 (general B.League development family; B.PREMIER calibration absent)
```

**Arithmetic:** normal CDF directly from SciPy in a coherent bivariate normal score proxy, using independent margin and total coordinates (not a possession-level joint grid), without integer discretisation or an explicit real-overtime branch. Because the two coordinate distributions share the same expected score centre, each side's and each total's complement agree. Continuity and OT approximation are a real limitation. The probability figures are illustrative only, not independently validated.

| Market | Numeric diagnostic | Valid v24.15 `p_card` |
|---|---:|---|
| Akita ML (informational) | 33.6% | NOT_ESTIMATED |
| Ibaraki ML (informational) | 66.4% | NOT_ESTIMATED |
| Akita −1.5 | 30.0% | NOT_ESTIMATED |
| Ibaraki +1.5 | 70.0% | NOT_ESTIMATED |
| Over 167.5 | 53.1% | NOT_ESTIMATED |
| Under 167.5 | 46.9% | NOT_ESTIMATED |

The full-game total/handicap includes OT as a contract definition; arithmetic uses historical final scores, but **does not model OT as a separate period**. Such a proxy is not eligible as a kit-certified v24.15 primary forecast.

**Scenario sensitivity:** Season-only cross-match centre 167.4 / Akita −5.69 margin after same home allowance -> Ibaraki +1.5 ≈68.5%, Over 167.5 ≈49.8%. L10-season blended margin −6.34 ±2 gives Ibaraki +1.5 about **65.2–74.5%**. Total centre 168.88 ±4 gives Over ≈**44.1–62.0%**. These sensitivity widths/weight assumptions are not outcome-derived calibration. Exact probability bands are unavailable.

## A4. Arithmetic — independent illustrative diagnostic, not repository model execution

Current team points from first four/three results and available nine-team 2026–27 league standings were independently reconciled. The complete indexed standings snapshot indicated cumulative WNBL points-for 3,434 across 42 team-games = 21 fixtures, league mean total 163.52, average team score 81.7619. This league mean is **provisional** because the provider's direct page sometimes renders an earlier snapshot; recompute from the official full 21-game ledger before any formal reference freeze. Standings snapshot: https://www.fanziz.com/basketball/series/wnbl-2627/points-table. Match-result corroboration: https://api.thesportsdb.com/season/5113-australian-wnbl/2026-2027.

Direct two-sided offence/defence mean diagnostic: Perth=(87.33+89.75)/2=88.54; Bendigo=(78.25+88.00)/2=83.13; total=171.67; Perth margin=5.42. This treats only three/four games as fully trustworthy and therefore is not the selected estimator.

**Shrinkage demonstrator:** fixed prior equivalent to `k=5` league games, not fitted or validated. Perth weight `wP=3/(3+5)=0.375`, Bendigo weight `wB=4/(4+5)=0.4444`. Let `L=163.5238/2=81.7619` team points. For each score, add its own shrunk offence departure and opponent shrunk defence departure. Split an **assumed** home advantage of +1.5 points in margin into +0.75/-0.75, taken for scenario illustration from the repository's FIBA40 family home-prior +1.49 (not WNBL-validated):

- `E(Perth)=L+wP×(87.333−L)+wB×(89.75−L)+0.75 = 88.1515`.
- `E(Bendigo)=L+wB×(78.25−L)+wP×(88−L)−0.75 = 81.7903`.
- `E(total)=169.9418`; `E(Perth margin)=+6.3611`.

Width floor proxy: `sigma_margin=12.76×1.10=14.036` and `sigma_total=16.82×1.10=18.502` from `PARAMETERS.md` FIBA40 family residual widths. WNBL-specific residuals, covariance, possessions and efficiency are NOT_ESTIMATED. For an illustrative normal marginal only: `P(Perth ML)≈Phi(6.3611/14.036)=0.6748`, `P(Perth -10.5)≈Phi((6.3611-10.5)/14.036)=0.3840`, `P(Bendigo +10.5)≈0.6160`, `P(Over 167.5)≈Phi((169.9418-167.5)/18.502)=0.5525`, `P(Under 167.5)≈0.4475`.

**Limitations:** these normal probabilities are hand-calculated (no MC or fitted engine), and are **not** published p_card or qualified probabilities. A fully coherent joint team-score distribution, actual WNBL possession-based ORtg/DRtg, covariance, overtime branch, confirmed lineup-specific effects and precision audit have not been computed. An approximation to completed-game averages is not an explicitly simulated overtime process. Under the repository, numerical gate, pair rank and joint-failure results are NOT_ASSESSABLE.

## A5. Complete supplied-contract assessment and sensitivity

| Scenario | Expected Perth | Expected Bendigo | Mean total | Mean home margin | Indicative P(Bendigo +10.5) | Indicative P(Over 167.5) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| More current-season weight (`k=3`) | 89.86 | 82.12 | 171.99 | +7.74 | 57.8% | 59.6% |
| Base illustrative (`k=5`) | 88.15 | 81.79 | 169.94 | +6.36 | 61.6% | 55.2% |
| More league shrinkage (`k=7`) | 87.09 | 81.61 | 168.69 | +5.48 | 64.0% | 52.6% |
| More shrinkage (`k=10`) | 86.08 | 81.45 | 167.53 | +4.63 | 66.2% | 50.1% |

**Availability conditional sensitivity, NOT empirical effects:** shifting Bendigo's base centre down 3 points (e.g., less creator/bench availability) changes its indicative +10.5 cover to 53.2% and Over to 48.8%. A −5-point Bendigo change yields about 47.6% and 44.5%, reversing the cover preference. A +3 change raises them to 69.4% and 61.6%. These are thought experiments, not estimates that an individual absence is worth 3 or 5 points.

**Interpretation:** Lynx winner is the broad directional call. Bendigo +10.5 is the leading conditional spread lean if the last-game rotation is retained; it is not a strong unconditional play because the exact-day absences are disputed. At 167.5, competing current-season rates and six older H2Hs do not support a stable total lean.

## A2. Exact contract freeze and definitions

| Exact contract | Settlement quantity and rule | Favours | Main failure mechanism |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mobis +3.0 | Mobis final points minus Goyang final points +3, including all overtime. W if Mobis wins or loses by 1–2; PUSH if loses by 3; L if loses by ≥4 | Mobis' home resilience / Goyang import vacancy | Goyang perimeter shooting runs away with game |
| Goyang −3.0 | Goyang final points minus Mobis final points −3, including overtime. W if Goyang wins by ≥4; PUSH if wins by 3 | Goyang offence / Mobis depleted depth | Spellman dominates and Mobis contain Lee Jung-hyun |
| Over 175.5 | Both teams' final score sum ≥176 including overtime | 3-point volume / weak defence / late fouling | Lower quality possessions with one foreign player each |
| Under 175.5 | Both teams' final score sum ≤175 including overtime | Reduced import depth / Mobis' 3P suppression | Goyang's high-volume 3P shooting or overtime |

Both handicap contracts refer to the same exact integer margin and are strict complements except for a shared push at exactly 3 points. Over and Under 175.5 have no integer push.

Spellman played 40 minutes against Anyang on November 8 and scored 33 points. Long exposure presents fatigue and late-foul risk.

- **Goyang offensive form:** Lee Jung-hyun scored in double figures in nine consecutive games after his opening four; approximately 21.0 points and 3.7 threes in those nine. Goyang had made ≥10 threes in each of 13 games, averaging 14.6 makes at 36.5%; Mobis had allowed 8.6 made threes per game (league minimum), but surrendered 16 in the October H2H.

Replacements cannot automatically be treated as zero contributors.

## A4. Numerical attempts and reference suitability

### Historical repo league profile vs current league

The repository's 22 Oct 2026 KBL historical parameter row states mean total 159.39, home margin +1.18, residual margin SD 12.61 and total SD 16.82 points (`PARAMETERS.md`, Part A). However, the October-November 2026–27 league is scoring more: summing the published team *points-for* in the 10-team current standings (10,466 points across 122 team games, equivalent to 61 scheduled games) yields 171.57 points per game. That aggregate is a cross-sectional **approximation**, not a validated point-in-time official league baseline. Data: https://www.linevs.com/stats/?left_country=South+Korea&left_league=KBL&tab=teams . Source table may be refreshed after the issue horizon, so prospective admissibility is **not established**.

The 12/13-game team scoring midpoint is:
- Mobis expected from own offense versus opponent allowance = (83.5 + 90.8) / 2 = **87.15**.
- Goyang expected from own offense versus opponent allowance = (89.6 + 88.7) / 2 = **89.15**.
- Exploratory total = **176.30** points; Goyang expected lead **2.00** points, before proper opponent strength, home, player availability and current 3P effects.

Recent-form (L5) midpoint, highly exposed to pre-injury regimes:
- Mobis (79.0 + 99.6) / 2 = 89.3; Goyang (95.4 + 89.2) / 2 = 92.3; exploratory total 181.6 and Goyang +3.0.

Current home/away alternate midpoint based on published rounded splits:
- Mobis home 84.0 scored, Goyang away 88.9 conceded → Mobis 86.45;
- Goyang away 85.9 scored, Mobis home 88.4 conceded → Goyang 87.15;
- Alternative total 173.60 and Goyang +0.70, before player revisions.

Thus even **before injury adjustment**, reasonable simple calculations span totals ~173.6–181.6 and away leads ~0.7–3.0. Because the available data do not establish a measured effect size for a foreign-player absence or a validated matchup-specific pace × efficiency distribution, do **not** arbitrarily subtract a fixed number of points and call the result the `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1` forecast.

### Transparency-only numeric calculation (NOT PRIMARY)

As a **demonstration only**, treating integer margin and total as marginal Normal variables around the *unadjusted season midpoint*, widening historical KBL residual SDs by 10% to represent uncertainty, gives margin SD ~13.87, total SD ~18.50. The probabilities below are not a complete joint possession-grid forecast; margin/total covariance, discrete overtime outcome paths, adjusted player minutes and calibration are missing. Approximate integer continuity cells for the 3-point spread are used, but are not validated model probabilities.

| Demonstration | Conditional decisive estimate | Limitation |
|---|---:|---|
| Goyang match win (winner margin ≥1) | ≈54.3% | simple unadjusted score-margin model |
| Mobis match win | ≈45.7% | same model, no team draw after OT |
| Mobis +3.0 cover given decisive | ≈53.0% | 2.9% illustrative exact-three PUSH mass |
| Goyang −3.0 cover given decisive | ≈47.0% | same push mass |
| Over 175.5 | ≈51.7% | unadjusted total normal, without availability |
| Under 175.5 | ≈48.3% | complement |

These approximate figures should **not** be used as final rank or confidence numbers: the present roster changes could move them by several percentage points, and the framework's issuance/freeze gates have not run. Strict publishable `p_card` for all contracts remains `NOT_ESTIMATED`.

- **Venue:** Ulsan Dongchun Gymnasium, Mobis home. Current home/away marks entering game: Mobis 2–6 home, Goyang 1–6 away. Quantified home-court delta unresolved.
- **Tempo / scoring regime:** current KBL scoring visibly higher than historical repo aggregate. Do not interchange older historical parameters with a point-in-time current league without documenting population drift.
- **In-game contingencies:** long stretches without a second import, O'Bryant foul trouble, Spellman fatigue and opponent-adjusted 3-point accuracy are the most important uncertain forks.

- Mobis +3.0 and Goyang −3.0 are opposite outcomes conditioned on the shared point-margin push at −3; they do not form independent selections.

- Side and total are **correlated through import absences, shooting variance and foul/OT exposure**; no joint model was constructed, so the exact joint failure and derived two-leg safe-pair claims are `NOT_COMPUTED`.
- The illustrative normal margin/total computations lack an explicit KBL possession grid and calibrated overtime branch. No false 100,000-run simulation, fitted model or pregame freeze is claimed.
- The framework's rank-1 threshold (0.65) cannot be met by any *supported* adjusted numeric record here. `NO_STRONG_PICK / ABSTAIN`.

## A5. Reference stage, score process, exact arithmetic and sensitivity

**Official repository `ml` reference:** `NOT_RUN`, because no checked-out writable repository and no authorized writer. NHL requires a league-only baseline, not an unpromoted fitted-model reference. Published `PARAMETERS.md` Part A NHL family has regular-season mean total **6.23**, raw total SD 2.30, residual total SD 2.30 and residual margin SD 2.55. Do not label the following `p_card` or `BASELINE_P`; no exact registered reference receipt was executed.

**Transparent Wolfram numerical diagnostic (run, but never as the repository model):**

- Official season scoring GF/GA: COL 72/18 GF, 55/18 GA; TOR 64/19 GF, 63/19 GA. The two cross-team unadjusted mids are `muCOL = (72/18 + 63/19)/2 = 3.657895` and `muTOR = (64/19 + 55/18)/2 = 3.211988`.
- Declared **illustrative**, untrained shrink coefficient 0.50 toward league-neutral 6.23/2 = 3.115 per team; illustrative Toronto home-edge +0.13 net (±0.065): `lambda_COL = 3.321447`, `lambda_TOR = 3.228494` goals in a *regulation proxy* model. The season scores include some OT; hence treating them as clean regulation rates is an approximation, not a correct issued primary model.
- For each 60-minute score `i,j` in 0..23, assign `p_ij=Poisson(lambda_COL,i)*Poisson(lambda_TOR,j)`. For each tie `i=j`, allocate one additional deciding goal to COL or TOR at a declared **unvalidated 50/50** OT/SO decision, on endpoints `(i+1,j)` and `(i,j+1)` respectively. No independent market quotes, no outcomes and no fitted/retrained engine used. Tail (24+ goals) contributes below the reported precision and was separately checked at 0..29. A separate pull-goalie/empty-net state was **not identified**, so the resulting puckline mass is illustrative and *not* an issued rank.
- Probability mass: `P(COL ML)=0.514202`, `P(TOR ML)=0.485798`, `P(TOR+1.5)=0.713790`, `P(COL−1.5)=0.286210`, `P(O6.5)=0.530312`, `P(U6.5)=0.469688`. Pair sums = 1 to numerical tolerance.
- Regulation tie mass `0.159070`; model final means COL `3.4010` and TOR `3.3080` (total `6.7090`, margin `0.0930`). This is **not a calibrated score forecast**.
- Goalie quality and injury differences are directional evidence but have `MEASURED_EFFECT_NOT_AVAILABLE`; neither is silently converted to a numeric goal shift.

| Explicit non-calibrated scenario | P(COL ML) | P(TOR +1.5) | P(O6.5) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Pure league prior with Toronto home edge | 48.0% | 74.8% | 48.1% |
| 50% team-season shrink (illustrative main diagnostic) | 51.4% | 71.4% | 53.0% |
| Unshrunk cross-matched season scoring | 54.7% | 67.9% | 57.8% |
| Mechanical COL goalie advantage, arbitrary ±0.2-goal shift | 57.5% | 65.9% | 53.0% |

Scenario coefficients are analyst assumptions, NOT empirically estimated confidence limits. The historical league-only prior itself is stale relative to exact Nov 2026 opponent-adjusted league population; do not claim an exact-row verified baseline. No source supports converting Bobrovsky's .886 vs Blackwood's .901 save rate directly into a guaranteed goal delta.

# v24.15 retro-converted research package — six events, no settlement

Created `2026-11-11T08:43:39+00:00` UTC against read-only GitHub HEAD `4d8bd1771d20974bcbfc7c694a009cd44e95a693`.

This package includes six historically faithful, A1–A9-complete v24.15 **format reconstructions**, not v24.15 formally admitted or prospectively issued forecasts. The original numeric diagnostics are retained as legacy values and are not promoted to primary `p_card`. No outcomes, results, settlement or grading were consulted. No GitHub writes.

- `CONTRACT_COVERAGE_UNSETTLED.csv`: one row for each exact supplied or derived informational contract; all primary p and W/P/L states unsupported and ungraded.
- `reconstructions/`: six converted Markdown dossiers, each with the full corresponding original report appended.
- `original_reports/`: byte-for-byte original individual reports.
- `CONVERSION_MANIFEST.json`: conversion provenance and original/converted SHA-256 values.
- `SUMMARY.md`: six-match table.
- `research/active_minis/20261109R3NEXT.md` (in ZIP): chat-local copy with section H added; original authoritatively published mini and footer not touched.
- `20261109R3NEXT_BEFORE_RETROSPECTIVE_CONVERSION.md`: exact local mini immediately before this append.

## Compatibility limits

No immutable event manifests or repository EV2 keys, no actual v24.15 L1–L5 accepted returns, no exact code-reproduced v24.15 SR-KIT receipts, no original pre-exposure SINGLE/FIVE freezes, no confirmed time-of-issue publication proofs, no certified source-access snapshot at original retrieval. A historical conversion cannot manufacture these retrospectively. No result/score/winner/grade is added. If later settlement is requested, keep it separate as historical diagnostic grading; do not change original timestamps or call these prospective predictions.

## A4. Numerical method and sensitivity
**Verified benchmark source:** Tennis Abstract WTA Elo, updated 9 November 2026: Hercog overall Elo 1528.3 and cElo 1510.8; Lepchenko overall Elo 1547.9 and cElo 1515.3. Under PROBABILITY_TOOLKIT §6, blended surface ratings are 1519.55 Hercog and 1531.60 Lepchenko. Hence 

`p_ELO(Hercog) = 1 / (1 + 10^((1531.60-1519.55)/400)) = 0.4826656059`;
`p_ELO(Lepchenko) = 0.5173343941`.

**Critical distinction:** These are a rating-reference calculation only, **not** a frozen, issued `ANALYST_PRIMARY_V1` match probability, and not a coherent score-distribution output. Tennis Abstract's separately published tournament-draw benchmark indicates 50.6% Hercog and 49.4% Lepchenko, reinforcing that both ratings/benchmark mechanisms are effectively a coin flip rather than demonstrating a meaningful edge. Do not use independent predictors as features in the card. https://tennisabstract.com/reports/wta_elo_ratings.html ; https://www.tennisabstract.com/current/2026WTASopo125.html .

**Point-to-match / set-score tree:** NOT_COMPUTED. Current surface-, opponent- and event-level numerators and denominators for each player's serve and return points and validated opponent-adjusted hold rates are not available in comparable form. Official WTA site has some Lepchenko cumulative stats but Hercog 2026 match-level stats are unavailable there. A fabricated per-game hold, fitted shock width, or hard-coded set-score branch is prohibited. Tier C: reference NONE. League baseline at exact handicaps/totals: BASELINE_UNAVAILABLE. Adjustments: none numerically entered. Sensitivity: Hercog's better clay and recent record may exceed Lepchenko's small Elo edge; unverified physical condition or atypical altitude could reverse either qualitative direction. No numerical confidence interval is asserted.

Expected games Hercog/Lepchenko, expected total games, standard deviations, match game margin and probability of 3 sets: NOT_ESTIMATED. A benchmark winner-only number cannot identify any of these.

## Decision summary

- **Likely winner:** Perth Lynx, direction supported by stronger W–L record and home court, but exact official model probability `NOT_ESTIMATED`.
- **Highest indicative contract:** Bendigo Spirit **+10.5**, *tentative, low-to-medium confidence*. The unqualified shrunk-scores diagnostic gives ~61.6% cover versus Perth −10.5 ~38.4%. This is **not a certified success probability**; it is highly sensitive to Bendigo's final active roster.
- **Total 167.5:** **no reliable side**. The simple current-season diagnostic gives Over ~55.2% / Under ~44.8%, while six prior H2Hs all finished Under and a plausible Bendigo availability reduction reverses the Over lean. **Abstain** for a strong total selection.
- **Formal Rank-1 status:** `NOT_ASSESSABLE / NO_STRONG_PICK`; no issued ranks, no pre-event frozen card.

## A4. Arithmetic — independent illustrative diagnostic, not repository model execution

Current team points from first four/three results and available nine-team 2026–27 league standings were independently reconciled. The complete indexed standings snapshot indicated cumulative WNBL points-for 3,434 across 42 team-games = 21 fixtures, league mean total 163.52, average team score 81.7619. This league mean is **provisional** because the provider's direct page sometimes renders an earlier snapshot; recompute from the official full 21-game ledger before any formal reference freeze. Standings snapshot: https://www.fanziz.com/basketball/series/wnbl-2627/points-table. Match-result corroboration: https://api.thesportsdb.com/season/5113-australian-wnbl/2026-2027.

Direct two-sided offence/defence mean diagnostic: Perth=(87.33+89.75)/2=88.54; Bendigo=(78.25+88.00)/2=83.13; total=171.67; Perth margin=5.42. This treats only three/four games as fully trustworthy and therefore is not the selected estimator.

**Shrinkage demonstrator:** fixed prior equivalent to `k=5` league games, not fitted or validated. Perth weight `wP=3/(3+5)=0.375`, Bendigo weight `wB=4/(4+5)=0.4444`. Let `L=163.5238/2=81.7619` team points. For each score, add its own shrunk offence departure and opponent shrunk defence departure. Split an **assumed** home advantage of +1.5 points in margin into +0.75/-0.75, taken for scenario illustration from the repository's FIBA40 family home-prior +1.49 (not WNBL-validated):

- `E(Perth)=L+wP×(87.333−L)+wB×(89.75−L)+0.75 = 88.1515`.
- `E(Bendigo)=L+wB×(78.25−L)+wP×(88−L)−0.75 = 81.7903`.
- `E(total)=169.9418`; `E(Perth margin)=+6.3611`.

Width floor proxy: `sigma_margin=12.76×1.10=14.036` and `sigma_total=16.82×1.10=18.502` from `PARAMETERS.md` FIBA40 family residual widths. WNBL-specific residuals, covariance, possessions and efficiency are NOT_ESTIMATED. For an illustrative normal marginal only: `P(Perth ML)≈Phi(6.3611/14.036)=0.6748`, `P(Perth -10.5)≈Phi((6.3611-10.5)/14.036)=0.3840`, `P(Bendigo +10.5)≈0.6160`, `P(Over 167.5)≈Phi((169.9418-167.5)/18.502)=0.5525`, `P(Under 167.5)≈0.4475`.

**Limitations:** these normal probabilities are hand-calculated (no MC or fitted engine), and are **not** published p_card or qualified probabilities. A fully coherent joint team-score distribution, actual WNBL possession-based ORtg/DRtg, covariance, overtime branch, confirmed lineup-specific effects and precision audit have not been computed. An approximation to completed-game averages is not an explicitly simulated overtime process. Under the repository, numerical gate, pair rank and joint-failure results are NOT_ASSESSABLE.

## A5. Complete supplied-contract assessment and sensitivity

| Scenario | Expected Perth | Expected Bendigo | Mean total | Mean home margin | Indicative P(Bendigo +10.5) | Indicative P(Over 167.5) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| More current-season weight (`k=3`) | 89.86 | 82.12 | 171.99 | +7.74 | 57.8% | 59.6% |
| Base illustrative (`k=5`) | 88.15 | 81.79 | 169.94 | +6.36 | 61.6% | 55.2% |
| More league shrinkage (`k=7`) | 87.09 | 81.61 | 168.69 | +5.48 | 64.0% | 52.6% |
| More shrinkage (`k=10`) | 86.08 | 81.45 | 167.53 | +4.63 | 66.2% | 50.1% |

**Availability conditional sensitivity, NOT empirical effects:** shifting Bendigo's base centre down 3 points (e.g., less creator/bench availability) changes its indicative +10.5 cover to 53.2% and Over to 48.8%. A −5-point Bendigo change yields about 47.6% and 44.5%, reversing the cover preference. A +3 change raises them to 69.4% and 61.6%. These are thought experiments, not estimates that an individual absence is worth 3 or 5 points.

**Interpretation:** Lynx winner is the broad directional call. Bendigo +10.5 is the leading conditional spread lean if the last-game rotation is retained; it is not a strong unconditional play because the exact-day absences are disputed. At 167.5, competing current-season rates and six older H2Hs do not support a stable total lean.

## A6. Adjustments, venue, fatigue, coaching and overlapping evidence

- **Home:** Perth's venue is confirmed; illustrative +1.5 final margin from FIBA40-family **scenario assumption**, not a measured WNBL adjustment ledger entry. Perth has only one current-season home game, so a stable home split is unavailable.
- **Rest/travel:** Perth last played Nov 3 (seven days), Bendigo Nov 7 (three days) and travels interstate. This may help Perth late; **no sourced WNBL effect size or quantified direction**; scenario only, not secretly added to the centre.
- **Availability:** projected absences already largely represented in current-season scores. Do not deduct missing Borlase/Rees again; avoid double counting. Late changes in Wehrung/Olechnowicz, Tolo's restriction and Perth's actual rotation require exact minutes to alter numbers defensibly.
- **Coaching:** Perth Ryan Petrik; Bendigo Kennedy Kereama. Sources: https://www.basketball.com.au/news/wnbl-rosters and https://bendigospirit.wnbl.com.au/news/kennedy-kereama-re-signs-as-head-coach-of-the-bendigo-spirit. Coaching effect NOT_ESTIMATED.
- **Weather:** indoor venue — not an operative scoring variable.
- **H2H:** six prior meetings offer caution for the total but have materially different scorers/usage; numerical weight ZERO here. Rivalry/history not a license for an extra point adjustment.

## A7. Dependence, baseline and model-shadow controls

In the absence of a supported joint team-score model, `P(both fail)` = **NOT_COMPUTED**. Illustrative margin and total normal marginals alone cannot certify that joint.

# KBL research-only draft — Ulsan Hyundai Mobis Phoebus v Goyang Sono Skygunners

**Research disposition:** `READ_ONLY_DRAFT / NOT_ISSUED / INFORMATION_INCOMPLETE`  
**Sport:** Basketball — South Korean KBL regular season, 2026–27  
**Estimated fixture:** 10 November 2026, 19:00 KST (21:00 AEDT / 10:00 UTC); Ulsan Dongchun Gymnasium, Ulsan, South Korea  
**Source timing:** Pre-game statistics and reports only; the research session began after the scheduled start, and actual first play was not independently verified. The source snapshots must not be presented as a verified pre-game-issued forecast.  
**User contracts:** Mobis +3.0 points; Goyang Sono −3.0 points; full-game Over/Under 175.5 points, all including overtime under BK-OT-1. Three-point handicap has a PUSH if Goyang wins by precisely three.

## A1. Identity, timing, event state

- Independent KBL organiser listing and Korean media identify the match as Ulsan Hyundai Mobis hosting Goyang Sono on 10 November at 19:00 Korea time, at Ulsan Dongchun Gymnasium.
- 10:00 UTC = 19:00 KST = 21:00 AEDT. The first time check in this analysis was 21:04:36 AEDT. The user supplied an estimated start and explicitly permits delays. Some web schedules continued to display *scheduled* and 0–0 placeholders, but these are not reliable first-play proof. The real opening tip is `NOT_VERIFIED`.
- No verified in-play score was used. Actual issue time relative to first play is `TIMING_UNVERIFIED` and this research cannot count as a clean, published pre-tip card.
- Identity source: https://www.kbl.or.kr/match/schedule ; official home-club https://phoebus.kbl.or.kr/ ; Korean reporting https://jumpball.co.kr/news/newsview.php?ncode=1065545227760436 ; corroboration https://sports.factnows.com/kbl/202611101066490163/ .

## A2. Exact contract freeze and definitions

| Exact contract | Settlement quantity and rule | Favours | Main failure mechanism |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mobis +3.0 | Mobis final points minus Goyang final points +3, including all overtime. W if Mobis wins or loses by 1–2; PUSH if loses by 3; L if loses by ≥4 | Mobis' home resilience / Goyang import vacancy | Goyang perimeter shooting runs away with game |
| Goyang −3.0 | Goyang final points minus Mobis final points −3, including overtime. W if Goyang wins by ≥4; PUSH if wins by 3 | Goyang offence / Mobis depleted depth | Spellman dominates and Mobis contain Lee Jung-hyun |
| Over 175.5 | Both teams' final score sum ≥176 including overtime | 3-point volume / weak defence / late fouling | Lower quality possessions with one foreign player each |
| Under 175.5 | Both teams' final score sum ≤175 including overtime | Reduced import depth / Mobis' 3P suppression | Goyang's high-volume 3P shooting or overtime |

Both handicap contracts refer to the same exact integer margin and are strict complements except for a shared push at exactly 3 points. Over and Under 175.5 have no integer push.
